| Sickness and vomiting | `Sick` has food poisoning and illness kinds, `Sick_resistance`, a delayed killer record; the vomiting countdown also confuses and stuns | One kind (food poisoning, curable by vomiting or by `makeSick(ctx, 0)`), the same countdown texts, recovery chance and death; no resistance; the vomiting countdown prints its texts but has no confusion or stun | Related statuses do not exist (ref: src/potion.c make_sick, src/timeout.c sickness_dialogue, vomiting_dialogue) |
| Killer names | Killers are formatted free text (`killed by a rotted newt corpse`, `Choked on a food ration`) with a format (KILLED_BY_AN, KILLED_BY, NO_KILLER_PREFIX) | Killers are message keys (`killer.rotted.nh:newt`, `killer.choked.nh:food_ration`, `killer.starvation`); the tombstone puts `a`/`an` in front of whatever the key says; a killer marked `!` (NO_KILLER_PREFIX: "killed while stuck in creature form", "shot herself with a death ray", "zapped herself with a wand of striking", "caught herself in her own fireball", "committed suicide", "went to heaven prematurely", "squished under a boulder", "killed herself by breaking a wand", "killed by petrification") is the whole text; texts with C's uhim()/uhis() follow her sex ("killed himself by breaking a wand", "his own pick-axe") | The game-over record carries a key, not formatted text (ref: src/end.c done_in_by) |
| Random objects | `mkobj()` rolls a class from `mkobjprobs` (weapon 10, armor 11, food 20, tool 8, gem 7, potion 16, scroll 16, spellbook 4, wand 4, ring 3, amulet 1) and then an object by `oc_prob` among the whole class | The class roll is the same; within a class the object is picked by `prob` weighted over the objects we have (our probs do not sum to 1000); `artif` makes a weapon (`!rn2(20 + 10 * nartifact_exist())`) or a piece of armor (`!rn2(40 + ...)`) a random artifact of its type as C (m20 step 1; only the artifacts whose base object exists: the two Orbs, the Magic Mirror and the Eyes are absent); random corpses use `rndmonst()` (falling back to any common monster) but corpse prob is 0 so they are not generated | Not every object exists (all weapons but the worm tooth, crysknife and boomerang since m11 step 1; 32 more armor rows, K- and C-rations and the bugle since m11 step 3) (ref: src/mkobj.c mkobj(), mksobj_init()) |
| Gems | The 22 gems, 9 glasses and gray stones; `setgemprobs()` by the level's ledger number; `randomize_gem_colors()` makes turquoise and aquamarine blue or green and fluorite violet, blue, white or green per game; gems can be rubbed on a touchstone, identified by a shopkeeper's (and a co-aligned unicorn's) reaction or kicked; `lift_object()` lifts a loadstone whatever it weighs; `readobjnam("glass")` picks a colour with `rn2(9)` | All of them, with objects.h prices, weights, colours and probabilities; setgemprobs() reads the level's number in the main dungeon and treats any other dungeon (the Mines) as past every cutoff, as C's ledger does; mkbox_cnts() turns rocks into gems and stones; the luckstone, the loadstone (made cursed; a cursed one cannot be dropped, thrown or put in a container; lifted regardless of weight) and the touchstone (a ring shopkeeper carries one, with no use); gem colours are fixed (no `randomize_gem_colors()`, so its draws are not made); no touchstone rubbing, no gem identification by shopkeepers or kicking (a unicorn's reaction to a thrown gem tells gems from glass only by its wording, as in 5.0: see Unicorns); a wish for "glass" picks a colour by probability (77 or 76 each) | Rubbing and the gem-buying reactions are their own features (ROADMAP) (ref: include/objects.h GEM(), ROCK(); src/o_init.c setgemprobs(), randomize_gem_colors(); src/objnam.c xname(), readobjnam_postparse2(); src/mkobj.c mksobj_init(), mkbox_cnts(); src/do.c canletgo(); src/pickup.c lift_object(), in_container(); src/shk.c get_cost(), set_cost()) |
| Level objects | `fill_ordinary_room()` rolls a monster, traps, gold, mimics, fountains, sinks, altars, graves, statues, the bonus supply chest / Mines food ration, a box or chest, engravings and random objects; `mktrap()` may leave a dead adventurer (`mktrap_victim()`: a corpse, cursed possessions, the trap's missile) on the first levels | Per room: monster (1 in 3), traps (`while (!rn2(x))`, x = 8 - depth / 6, at least 2), gold (1 in 3), a large box or chest (1 in `nroom * 5 / 2`, with `mkbox_cnts()` contents), objects (1 in 3, then 1 in 5 each further); a fountain (1 in 10, placed by `find_okay_roompos()`: not next to a door; 1 in 7 magic) after the gold; a statue (1 in 20, `mkcorpstat(STATUE, CORPSTAT_INIT)`: a random monster, maybe a spellbook inside) after the fountain; a sink and an altar (1 in 60 each, after the fountain, placed by `find_okay_roompos()`; the altar of a random alignment, not a shrine) (m12 step 5); graffiti (1 in `27 + 3 * depth`, after the box: a random engraving, MARK, on a floor square of the room, m16 step 6); a grave (1 in `80 - 2 * depth`, at least 2, after the altar: `mkgrave()`, m16 step 7, see Graves); mimics and the bonus items do not exist so they do not roll (and the bonus room is not chosen), and the RNG stream therefore differs from NetHack's. No trap victim is made (its `rnd(4)` roll is). Monsters, traps, gold, statues, boxes and objects use `somexyspace` (plain floor, never the stairs or a trap) | Those features and the victim's monsters (elves, humans, player monsters) do not exist (ref: src/mklev.c fill_ordinary_room(), mktrap(), mktrap_victim(), src/mkroom.c somexyspace()) |
| Random traps | `traptype_rnd()` picks one of the 25 trap kinds with level restrictions (rolling boulders and sleeping gas from level 2, landmines 6, webs 7...), fire traps only in Gehennom, holes only 1 time in 7, rerolling until a kind is allowed | The same roll and reroll loop, spiked pits and level teleporters from level 5, no teleportation traps or level teleporters on a no-teleport level (Sokoban); rolling boulder traps from level 2, land mines from level 6, webs from level 7 (m10; each with a giant spider made on it, also for a special level's random `des.trap()`; a web with no spider is allowed at any depth, as C's MKTRAP_NOSPIDERONWEB, though no level asks for one yet), statue traps from level 8 (7f-c: with their statue, see Statue traps), polymorph traps from level 8 (7f-d, see Polymorph); fire traps (m17 step 2) only in Gehennom, where `mktrap()` first makes a random trap a fire trap 1 time in 5 (`!rn2(5)`, drawn only there); a special level's stocked maze (`fill_empty_maze()`, sp_lev.c `rndtrap()`, which has no fire case) may make one on any level (the Castle's mazes); the 4 kinds this port lacks or never makes at random (magic portals, the vibrating square, trapped doors and chests) count as not allowed, so the 21 kinds we have (magic traps and anti-magic fields at any depth) are proportionally more common. A fire trap is C's (`trapeffect_fire_trap()`, `dofiretrap()`): the tower of flame, fire resistance, the maximum HP loss, burning armor and items, scrolls on the floor, the ice under it melting, a trapped box sitting in a pool boiling the water; monsters that resist fire do not mind one (`m_harmless_trap()`, no artifacts so never `defended()`), a slimed monster walks onto one next to it (`muse_unslime()`), jumping over one sets it off (`hurtle_step()`), `#untrap` cannot disable one; never under water (no Underwater) and no `shieldeff()`. A hole or trap door where one cannot fall through becomes a falling rock trap, and a pit or hole avoids a boulder's square, as in NetHack. No niches (`make_niches()`: trap doors and level teleporters behind them), but the vault teleporter's (`makevtele()`, see Vaults) | Only 21 trap kinds are made at random; no ordinary niches (ref: src/mklev.c traptype_rnd(), mktrap(), make_niches(); src/sp_lev.c rndtrap(); src/trap.c trapeffect_fire_trap(), dofiretrap(), m_harmless_trap(); src/muse.c munslime(), muse_unslime(); src/dothrow.c hurtle_step()) |
| Dungeon bottom | The Dungeons of Doom has 25 to 29 levels (`rn1(5, 25)`) with the rogue level, the Oracle, the big room, Medusa and the Castle placed in dungeon.lua's order, the Quest portal, Fort Ludios' portal and the Elemental Planes' connection; holes and trap doors are not made on its bottom level but the Castle's, which lead to the Valley of the Dead (`Can_fall_thru()`, `find_hell()`); a level teleport past its bottom goes to the Valley; Gehennom (20-24 levels) has the Valley, the demon lairs, the Wizard's Tower, the fake towers, the vibrating square level and the Sanctum, its levels are `hellfill.lua` mazes, and until the invocation the Sanctum cannot be reached by holes or teleports (`dng_bottom()`) | The main dungeon (`nh:main`, m15 step 9) has 25 to 29 levels with the Oracle (DL5-9), the big room (DL10-12, 40% of games), Medusa (1-4 levels above the bottom) and the Castle (the bottom), placed in dungeon.lua's order; m21: the Rogue level (DL15-18, first in that order) and Fort Ludios (its branch rolled on DL18-21, its portal in a vault from DL11 down to above Medusa: see those rows) are placed too, and the Quest portal and the Planes are left out and draw nothing. Gehennom (`nh:gehennom`) has `rn1(5, 20)` levels right below the Castle, joined to it with no stairs on the Castle (dungeon.lua's "no_down"), its first level's up stairs leading back to the Castle's east bank (its `up` teleport region); the Castle's trap doors drop the hero and monsters onto that first level, and a level teleport from the main dungeon past its bottom goes there too. m17 step 1: its eleven special levels (the Valley, the Sanctum, Juiblex, Baalzebub, Asmodeus, the three Wizard's Tower levels chained to the first, Orcus, the two fake towers) are placed at new game in dungeon.lua's order (Vlad's Tower is left out and draws nothing), but until each one's own step every level but the Valley (m17 step 2, see The Valley of the Dead), the lairs of Juiblex, Asmodeus and Baalzebub (m17 step 6, see Gehennom's lairs), Orcus Town (m17 step 7) and the Wizard's Tower and the fake towers (m17 step 8, see The Wizard's Tower) is built as Gehennom's filler (only the Sanctum is left), `hellfill.lua`'s level (`nh:hellfill`, m17 step 3, see Gehennom's filler), as its stand-in; the Sanctum stays out of reach as before the invocation (`dng_bottom()`: holes, trap doors and random level teleports stop on the level above it, a level teleport aimed at it says "Sorry..." and stops there; wizard mode's #levelport may go there, as C's wizard may), and the level above it has the vibrating square (hellfill.lua's `des.trap("vibrating square")`, at `pick_vibrasquare_location()`'s spot) instead of down stairs. Arriving on the Valley from outside Gehennom gives its three lines ("You arrive at the Valley of the Dead..."); every level change ends with the level temperature's lines (see Level temperature). Being on a Gehennom level other than the Valley, or answering yes at the Valley's gate ("Are you sure you want to enter?" on its down stairs), marks Gehennom entered (`gehennom_entered`, which stops prayer's castle-tune hint). In Gehennom random monsters follow `G_HELL`/`G_NOHELL` and leave lawful kinds out (`uncommon()`, `rndmonst_adj()`, `mkclass()`, `mkclass_poly()`) and prayer is C's (see Prayer). A game saved before m15 keeps its endless main dungeon: for holes, trap doors and level teleports its bottom is level 25 (`DUNGEON_BOTTOM`), none are generated from level 25 on (they become falling rock traps), a fall from above stops at 25 and a hole placed below it by a mod leads one level down | The rest of Gehennom (the special levels' own maps: m17's later steps; Vlad's Tower, the invocation and the Sanctum), the Quest and the Planes are later milestones (ROADMAP "From m15"); the layout is in the save, so old games cannot gain the new levels (a game started before m17 keeps its stub Gehennom without special levels, and its last level becomes unreachable) (ref: dat/dungeon.lua; src/dungeon.c init_dungeons(), level_range(), place_level(), Can_fall_thru(), Can_dig_down(), Invocation_lev(), find_hell(); src/trap.c fall_through(), hole_destination(), clamp_hole_destination(), dng_bottom(), mlevel_tele_trap(); src/teleport.c level_tele(), random_teleport_level(); src/do.c goto_level(); src/mkmaze.c makemaz(), pick_vibrasquare_location()) |
| Branch stairs | Branch stairs that the hero has used are described with where they lead ("branch staircase down to the Gnomish Mines", `stairs_description()`); several branch kinds (`stair`, `no_up`, `no_down`, `portal`), at most one per level | Branch stairs look and are described like ordinary stairs. A branch is a staircase from its parent to the branch's entry level, down or (Sokoban) up, or (`stairs: 'child'`, m15) has no stairs on the parent's end (Gehennom's from the Castle). m21: Fort Ludios's `portal` branch is a `stairs: 'child'` branch whose rolled level gives only the draws and the depth; content keeps where its portal really went (`modData.nh.knox`), puts a hidden magic portal in that vault and the portal back on the fort's branch spot, and lifts the up stairs core makes there | No stair traversal memory; no core portal branch type yet (m21 plan Q4: a `stairs: 'none'` can come with the Quest) (ref: src/stairs.c stairs_description(), src/mklev.c place_branch(), mk_knox_portal()) |
| Cave maps | `join_map()` collects at most MAXNROFROOMS*2 (80) regions, leaving any further ones unjoined; an empty cave is kept; `finish_map()` lights `bg` cells too when `bg` is not rock; `walled` only grows walls into STONE | No region limit, so caves of any size are connected; a cave with fewer than 4 open cells (small maps only) is made again from the continuing RNG stream; a lit cave lights only `fg` cells and walls; `walled` grows walls into whatever `bg` is | Any map size must work; core does not know which terrains are rock (ref: src/mkmap.c join_map(), finish_map(); src/sp_lev.c wallify_map()) |
| The Gnomish Mines | 8-9 levels entered from DL2-4, with Minetown 3-6 levels in (temple, shops, watch) and Mines' End at the bottom; the dungeon is `mazelike` and lawful; every level but those is `minefill.lua` | The same layout rolls, with Minetown on Mines level 3 or 4 (see Minetown) and Mines' End on the last level (see Mines' End); every other level is `minefill`; the `mazelike` flag and the alignment do nothing yet; there are no hero races (the hero is always human), so the Mines' gnomes, dwarves and hobbits are always hostile, where NetHack makes most of them peaceful to a gnome or dwarf hero | Races are not implemented (ref: dat/dungeon.lua, dat/minetn-*.lua, dat/minend-*.lua, src/makemon.c peace_minded()) |
| Mines' End | Three variants, `rnd(3)`: the Mimic of the Mines (gem niches behind locked secret doors, four mimics posing as gray stones), the Gnome King's Wine Cellar (booze, a potion of object detection, a treasure chamber, engraved warnings, a teleport region) and the Catacombs (a maze walk, `nommap`, mummies, zombies, two vampires, floating eyes, two level teleporters on the luckstone and flint spots); an arrival room, non-diggable walls and the luckstone achievement | The three variants with their maps, regions, doors, stairs, fixed and random objects, traps and monsters; the shuffled niches, the not-cursed luckstone, minend-2's conditional locked door, the Catacombs maze and its two level teleporters (made after the monsters, so the random stream differs from NetHack's) are content fixups. The four mimics posing as a luckstone, loadstone, flint and touchstone are made after the objects of their niches (m13 step 3, `LevelContent.appearAs`). minend-2's teleport region holds random arrivals (m15 step 5). The wine cellar's two engraved signs are made by the fixup before the objects (m16 step 6). The two vampires (`V`) are back (m18 step 2). Left out: the arrival regions, non-diggable walls and the achievement. The wine cellar's potion of object detection is a random potion. `nommap` is a levelflags.ts flag that the scroll and spell of magic mapping obey (m17 step 2); `#wizmap` ignores it, as NetHack's does | No digging or object detection yet (ROADMAP) (ref: dat/minend-1.lua, dat/minend-2.lua, dat/minend-3.lua; src/sp_lev.c lspo_mazewalk(); src/mkmaze.c walkfrom()) |
| Mines filler | `minefill.lua` first fills the map with stone (its random lighting draws one `rn2(2)`), then makes the cave; and its last monster is `percent(50) and "h" or "G"`; `G` and `h` pick from gnomes, gnome lords, gnomish wizards, gnome kings, dwarves, dwarf lords, dwarf kings, hobbits, bugbears and mind flayers | No solidfill and no extra roll (the cave init alone makes the same map); the 1-2 boulders (75%) are made as in the lua file; the last monster is two independent 50% entries, an `h` and a `G` (so 0, 1 or 2 of them, 1 on average as before), since a LevelDef has no either/or; `G` and `h` pick only from the classes' monsters this port has (gnome, gnome lord, gnomish wizard, gnome king; dwarf, hobbit) | The LevelDef format has no alternatives; the other monsters do not exist yet (ref: dat/minefill.lua) |
| Debug: no monster generation | `iflags.debug_mongen` (set from the test suite's `nh.debug_flags({ mongen = false })`) makes every `makemon()` fail | `modData.nh.debugMongen` stops only the random 1-in-70 spawns (the roll is still drawn); tests use it so their outcome does not hang on the seed | Tests place their own monsters through makemon (ref: include/flag.h debug_mongen, src/makemon.c makemon()) |
| Monster starting gear | `m_initweap()`/`m_initinv()` give darts, slings, flint/rocks, crossbows, bows, arrows, pick-axes, candles, battle axes, two-handed swords, lucern hammers and random offensive/defensive/misc items (`rnd_offensive_item()` etc.); orcish arrows are poisoned | Darts, slings, rocks, flint, bows, crossbows and arrows are given as C does (only Uruk-hai get orcish bows with `m_initthrow` arrows, as in 5.0; an orc-captain is equipped as a Mordor orc or an Uruk-hai); since m11 step 1 the battle-axes, two-handed swords, lucern hammers, polearms, flails, silver sabers and elven weapons are given too, and since m11 step 3 the elven armor, the mercenaries' armor (with its creation rolls, which were not made before), the watchmen's tin whistle, the soldiers' K- and C-rations, their officers' bugle and the priests' cloaks of protection and magic resistance; an object a table lacks (a mod that removes it) is not given, but its pre-roll conditions and `mksobj` init rolls are made; since m14 step 7 the offensive, defensive and misc items are given as C does (`rnd_offensive_item()`, `rnd_defensive_item()`, `rnd_misc_item()` by `m_lev`, with their `mksobj` init; ordinary soldiers skip the defensive and misc rolls 12 times in 13), without Pestilence's potion of sickness or the vampire shapeshifters' exclusion (neither exists); gnome candles are rolled but not made, always with `rn2(60)` (NetHack rolls `rn2(20)` in the Mines while the level is made); ammo is never poisoned (the `!rn2(100)` roll is made). Monsters are given gear on every `makemon` (flag `noInventory` disables it); m17 step 4: Orcus's wand of death, the horned devil's trident (3 in 4) or bullwhip, Asmodeus's wands of cold and fire, a demon's blessed object cursed; m18 step 4: the balrog's bullwhip and broadsword, Dispater's wand of striking, Yeenoghu's flail, the ice devil's spear (1 in 4) | Gnome candles and poisoned ammo (ref: src/makemon.c m_initweap(), m_initinv(), m_initthrow(); src/muse.c rnd_defensive_item(), rnd_offensive_item(), rnd_misc_item()) |
| Zombies and mummies | A zombie's victim may rise as a zombie (5.0 `zombie_maker()`, zombification); zombies and mummies leave their race's old corpse (`undead_to_corpse()`), the ettin mummy an ettin corpse | No zombification. Zombies and mummies leave their race's corpse aged past TAINT_AGE (kobold, gnome, orc, dwarf, elf, human, giant and ettin; m10 step 4 added the human, elf and giant rows, never randomly generated, and the ettin). The water demon cannot summon (`msummon()`) | Zombification is not in the port (ref: src/mon.c undead_to_corpse(), make_corpse()) |
| Hiding monsters | A snake or spider (`S_SNAKE`, `S_SPIDER`) made while a level is created gets a random object on its square (`mkobj_at(RANDOM_CLASS)`) and hides under it (`hideunder()`); hiders (`M1_CONCEAL`, `M1_HIDE`) hide under objects or on ceilings as they move; eels hide in water; the hero hides as a form (`u.uundetected`) | m13 step 1: `mundetected` (`modData.nh.mundetected`) through the `nh:hiding` perception (telepathy still senses a hidden monster); level-made snakes and spiders hide under their object; a hider-underer stays on a pile it can hide under 9 times in 10 and hides again after it moves (`rn2(5)`), comes out when its object goes (`maybe_unhide_at()` after moving, teleporting, and at the start of its move as a guard for object paths that do not call it); piercers, trappers and lurkers above hide on floor squares when unwatched (`restrap()`, 1 in 3) and do not move while hidden; the hero returning to a level gives hiders a chance to hide (`hide_monst()` after `rnd(10)`); moving into a hidden hider-underer says "Wait!  There's a garter snake hiding under a dagger!" (blind, hallucinating and unseen variants); walking under a ceiling hider drops it ("A rock piercer suddenly drops from the ceiling!", a hard helmet takes the blow, `u.uac + 3 <= rnd(20)` misses, else `d(4,6)`; others attack by surprise or are surprised) and it moves next to her; an attack from hiding reveals it ("The garter snake was hidden under ...!"); searching, kicking, a forced fight and monster attacks bring them out ("Suddenly, you notice ..."). Eels (m15 step 2) hide only in water ("You see the giant eel dive under the water."): made with the level, after moving (`rn2(5)`), unwatched and away from her or fleeing (`!rn2(4)`), when she comes back to the level; they come out on land; walking into one in the water says "Wait!  There's a hidden monster there!", its attack from hiding "... was hidden under the water!" (when an object lies there, as C). Not ported: the hero hiding, Half_physical_damage (no source), Detect_monsters, the a11y monster notices, hiding monsters thrown at or hurtled into, revived and statue-animated hiders (they are never hidden when made), an Underwater hero seeing eels hide | Polymorphed hiding and those systems do not exist (ref: src/makemon.c makemon(); src/mon.c hideunder(), hide_monst(), maybe_unhide_at(), restrap(), movemon_singlemon(); src/monmove.c m_move(), postmov(), can_hide_under_obj(); src/hack.c domove_attackmon_at(), spoteffects(); src/uhitm.c attack_checks(); src/mhitu.c hitmu(); src/detect.c mfind0(); src/dokick.c kick_monster(); src/mhitm.c mattackm(); src/restore.c getlev()) |
| Mimics (m13) | Small, large and giant mimics pose as objects, furniture or (the Wizard) monsters; `set_mimic_sym()` picks the shape, `display_monster()` draws it and the hero's map keeps it (furniture overrides `lastseentyp`); a boulder or door disguise blocks light; bumping, opening, unlocking, kicking, searching, fighting or hurting one reveals it (`stumble_onto_mimic()`, `seemimic()`), a sticky one holds her, an unwatched one takes a disguise again (`restrap()`) and a disguised one never moves | Ported with C's rolls: `set_mimic_sym()` whole (the top object; a closed door on door, wall and secret squares; maze levels' statue; boulders outside rooms; gold in zoos and vaults; Delphi; altars of a random alignment in temples; shops' strange object or stock; syms[]/furnsyms[] elsewhere, with a throwaway object made and removed for a class's otyp; the monster shapes of statues, figurines, corpses, eggs and tins), `restrap()` and `hide_monst()`'s second try, the movemon_singlemon()/disturb() checks, `attack_checks()`'s three mimic cases, `that_is_a_mimic()`'s lines ("Wait!  That's a giant mimic!", "That closed door actually is ...", "That dagger is ...", "Those gold pieces are ..."), `stumble_onto_mimic()` with sticking, `stumble_on_door_mimic()` for #open and lock picking, `mfind0()`, kicks, `wakeup()`, monster fights, `newcham()`, chat silence, `monster_nearby()` and running; hallucination shows a random object (furniture stays), telepathy the mimic. Differences: a disguise blocks no light (sight blockers are boulders only); the look command on a furniture mimic also names the real floor under it (core's terrain memory is not overridden); the position prompt's monster list (`m`) still includes a disguised mimic (the view has no flag that would not itself leak it); a statue disguise is named "statue" (not "statue of a jackal"); the health food store's slime mold is a lump of royal jelly (no slime mold); there is no rogue level, mimic keeping a key, or zap-specific reveal line (zaps reveal through `wakeup()`); shop, door and special-level mimics are made (m13 step 3: see Shopkeepers and stock, Level generation, Sokoban, Mines' End) and a cursed object detector shows object mimics; since m18 step 3 the ring of protection from shape changers reveals every mimic on the level when put on (asleep unless it posed as a monster; `normal_shape()`), keeps new ones (`set_mimic_sym()`, sp_lev's `appear_as`, the Wizard's clone) from taking a disguise, and lets sleeping ones take one again when it comes off (`restartcham()`); cancellation reveals one too | Decision 3 of the m13 plan: disguises through the perception `shownAs`, with core remembering the shown id (ref: src/makemon.c set_mimic_sym(); src/mon.c seemimic(), restrap(), hide_monst(), wakeup(); src/uhitm.c attack_checks(), that_is_a_mimic(), stumble_onto_mimic(); src/lock.c stumble_on_door_mimic(); src/display.c display_monster()) |
| Unicorns (m13) | White, gray and black unicorns: a co-aligned one is made peaceful, stays off the hero's lines (NOTONL) and, with nowhere to go, teleports half the time; it wants only gemstones; killing a co-aligned one costs 5 Luck; its corpse leaves a unicorn horn (a revived one's crumbles half the time, or is `degraded_horn`); applying a horn cures timed troubles or, cursed, causes one (sickness, blindness, confusion, stun, vomiting, hallucination, deafness); a gem thrown at a unicorn is caught (`gem_accept()`: Luck by alignment and knowledge, glass known or named is refused); pets and monsters apply horns; a unicorn sacrificed on an altar | m13 step 4: the three rows and the horn (prob 0) from the converter; makemon()'s peace, mfndpos()'s NOTONL (for every mover, pets too, as `mon_allowflags()` gives it) and the noteleport-level fallback, m_move()'s `rn2(2)` rloc(), mon_would_take_item(), xkilled()'s guilt, make_corpse()'s horn and crumbling (`mrevived` is kept for it, set by revive()), use_unicorn_horn() and gem_accept() as 5.0 has them (5.0's horn cures properties only, no lost attributes; gem_accept() identifies nothing). Differences: deafness does not exist, so a cursed horn's seventh harm says "Nothing seems to happen." and deafness is never a trouble to fix; the goop on her face (m18 step 6) is not a trouble a horn fixes; `degraded_horn` is not kept (only `poly_obj()` reads it); individual object names do not exist (only #call names count as guesses); `mavenge` is not kept; pets and monsters do not use horns (dogmove.c, muse.c), nor does a polymorphed hero (#monster); sacrifice does not exist (see ROADMAP); `mrevived`'s experience and kill-count reductions are not ported | Those systems do not exist (ref: src/makemon.c makemon(); src/mon.c mon_allowflags(), mfndpos(), xkilled(), make_corpse(); src/monmove.c m_move(), mon_would_take_item(); src/apply.c use_unicorn_horn(); src/dothrow.c thitmonst(), gem_accept(); src/zap.c revive()) |
| Long worms (m13) | Long worms grow a tail of segments that follows the head, grows (`wgrowtime`) and shrinks; a tail square is the worm (`m_at()`); a blow on the tail may cut it (the end lost, or a new worm cloned from the part behind the cut); the tail bites through `wormhitu()`; teleporting, polymorph, genocide, death and level changes handle the tail; a long worm leaves a worm tooth, which enchant weapon turns into a crysknife (a crysknife put down reverts); `notonhead` changes some attacks on a tail (engulfing or hugging it misses, mirrors, artifacts) | m13 step 5: the three rows, the worm tooth and the crysknife (prob 0) from the converter. Each visible segment is an entity of def `nh:long_worm_tail` with only a position (no monster component), linked in `modData.nh.worms`/`wormTail`; content's creature-at-a-square lookup (`mAt()`) answers the worm on a tail square for melee, Fight, kicks, throws, polearms, zaps, rays, explosions, digging, door closing, placement and occupancy; makemon()'s `get_wormno()` (31 worms a level) and `rn2(5)` segments (MM_NOTAIL as a `noTail` option), `place_worm_tail_randomly()` with `rnd_nextto_goodpos()`, `worm_move()` (growth time `rn1(10, 2)` scaled by speed, the HP limit by length), `worm_nomove()`, `cutworm()` from her melee (blades and axes cut better), thrown weapons and polearms, `worm_cross()` for her and for `mfndpos()`, `wormhitu()` (as in 5.0, it measures from the head: only a worm whose head is also next to her bites again), rloc's tail re-laying, `wormgone()` on death, genocide and polymorph (a new long worm form gets a tail; the same zap does not polymorph it again through its tail), migration dropping the tail and growing it back on arrival, monster detection showing the tail, `canseemon()`'s `worm_known()`, chwepon()'s tooth and crysknife (a stack fuses) and `obj_no_longer_held()`'s revert in place_object(). Differences: monsters (pets, Conflict fights) never attack a worm through its tail and the hero does not swap places with a peaceful worm's tail; core moves a tail segment, not the worm, off the stairs she arrives on; `notonhead` effects are not ported (there are no artifacts or mirrors here; engulfing or hugging a tail hits the worm); a polymorphed hero's weapon attacks and a kicked object do not cut; the changes of a tooth or crysknife on a shop bill (`alter_cost()`, `costly_alteration()`) are not made; `size_wseg()` (#stats) does not exist; a tame worm's tail is not drawn as tame; segments may stand apart from the head and each other after the head moved without them (a pet swap, the stairs), as in C | No artifacts, mirrors or shop re-pricing of changed objects (ref: src/worm.c; src/makemon.c makemon(), clone_mon(); src/monmove.c m_move(), dochug(); src/mon.c mfndpos(), m_detach(), newcham(), make_corpse(); src/hack.c test_move(); src/uhitm.c known_hitum(); src/dothrow.c thitmonst(); src/teleport.c rloc_to_core(); src/dog.c mon_arrive(), mon_leave(); src/zap.c bhitm(); src/wield.c chwepon(); src/do.c obj_no_longer_held(); src/display.c detect_wsegs()) |
| Figurines (m13) | Random figurines are of a slightly harder monster (`rndmonnum_adj(5, 10)`, never human), blessed or cursed 1 time in 4, with a gender; applying one sets it down ("You set the figurine beside you and it transforms.") and `make_familiar()` makes its monster tame, as made or hostile by BUC (80/10/10), named after the figurine; an extinct species' figurine turns to dust; a cursed carried one transforms on its own (`rnd(9000) + 200` turns; "You see a kitten drop out of your pack!"); stone to flesh animates one | Ported: `mksobj_init()` FIGURINE, xname()'s "figurine of a ...", the wish, `use_figurine()` with `figurine_location_checks()`, `make_familiar()`'s figurine branch, the transform timer (`attach_fig_transform_timeout()` from `carry_obj_effects()` in `addinv()`/`mpickobj()`, `curse()`; stopped by `freeinv()`, `uncurse()`, `bless()`, applying), `fig_transform()` with its messages and `rnd(5000)` re-arm, `stone_to_flesh_obj()` FIGURINE, `xkilled()`'s small-monster exception, `poly_obj()` keeping the monster. Differences: no gender (figurines, like statues and corpses, roll none, so no `rn2(2)`), no object names to pass on, no pools, air or water levels ("release the figurine" never), no minions; the BUC hooks are at the curse/uncurse sites that can reach a carried figurine (thrones and spells' rndcurse(), remove curse, prayer, fountains, cancellation); a figurine on another level transforms when its timer comes due (C runs the level's timers on arrival), silently | Those systems do not exist (ref: src/mkobj.c mksobj_init(), curse(), uncurse(), bless(); src/apply.c use_figurine(), fig_transform(); src/dog.c make_familiar(); src/timeout.c attach_fig_transform_timeout(); src/invent.c carry_obj_effects(), freeinv_core(); src/zap.c stone_to_flesh_obj()) |
| Golems (m13) | Straw, paper, rope, gold, leather, wood, flesh, clay, stone, glass and iron golems have fixed hit points (`golemhp()`, also for a golem form and level drain) and leave their material (`make_corpse()`): iron chains, glass, rocks, a statue, wooden weapons and a boomerang, leashes, whips and grappling hooks, leather armor and saddles, gold, blank scrolls; `golemeffects()`/`ugolemeffects()`: lightning heals flesh golems, fire heals iron golems, and slows; paper and straw golems burn away, iron golems rust and wood and leather golems rot to pieces; cancellation erases a clay golem's word; stone to flesh and stoning switch stone and flesh golems; polypiling makes golems | m13 step 6: the eleven rows from the converter, with all of that. | Blobs, puddings and elementals (m10) | Gray oozes, brown and black puddings and green slime leave a glob (`make_corpse()`), which melds with nearby globs and is eaten specially; purple worms (and babies) attack an adjacent shrieker without Conflict (`mm_aggression()`: ALLOW_M for hostiles); `m_respond()` also has Medusa's gaze and the Erinyes' aggravation, and a boomerang passing a shrieker makes it respond; the earth elemental walks through rock (`passes_walls`, ALLOW_WALL) | The gray ooze and brown pudding leave nothing; a shrieker within one square shrieks each action ("The shrieker shrieks.", `stop_occupation()`, 1 in 10 a random monster, 1 in 13 of those a purple worm or, when too strong for the level, a baby purple worm; then `aggravate()`), and when a wand wakes it; a brown pudding hit hand to hand with a wielded iron or metal object (not ammo or missiles), by the hero or a monster, divides (`clone_mon()`: half its HP to the clone, conditions copied, a peaceful or tame one's clone stays so `rn2(max(2 + Luck, 2))` of the time); purple worms never seek out shriekers (only pets fight monsters); the earth elemental and the black pudding came in m13 step 8 (the black pudding leaves nothing and divides as the brown pudding) | No glob objects yet (taken default); hostile monsters have no ALLOW_M path in `mfndpos()`; no Erinyes or boomerangs (Medusa's gaze: m15 step 6); wall-walking in `mfndpos()` since m12 step 4 (ref: src/mon.c m_respond(), m_respond_shrieker(), mm_aggression(), make_corpse(); src/makemon.c clone_mon(); src/uhitm.c hmon_hitmon_splitmon(); src/mhitm.c mattackm()) |

| Engulfing | Engulfers (`gulpmu()`) swallow the hero onto their square for `u.uswldtim` turns, the map is replaced by the swallow box, only the engulfer can attack her, its damage type works each turn (total digestion, moisture, debris, acid, blinding dust, shocks, frost, fire, disease, energy drain), she is expelled when time runs out, it is hurt or flees, or the wand of opening, digging or life saving lets her out; from inside every blow hits, wands and spells hit it (`bhitm()`, rays "rip into" it), thrown and dropped objects end up in it, she can take them back out (`loot_mon()`), floors and stairs are out of reach, an angry god's lightning hits it; | Stoning | A cockatrice's hiss starts a five-turn petrification (`Stoned`, `stoned_dialogue()`), a new moon makes it certain, touching or eating cockatrices petrifies at once, monsters turn into statues holding their inventory (`monstone()`) or, small ones, rocks, golems turn into stone golems (`mon_to_stone()`), vampires revert (`vamp_stone()`), monsters cure themselves with lizard corpses or acidic potions (`munstone()`), falling while wielding a cockatrice corpse touches it (`selftouch()`), the hero leaves a statue in bones, cockatrice eggs petrify | m9 step 6 ports the hiss (both directions), the countdown with its lines, helplessness and the delayed killer, cures (lizard or acidic corpse, prayer's TROUBLE_STONED, life saving), the passive against bare hands and barefoot kicks, touching (picking up, looting), wielding, hitting with, throwing and kicking a cockatrice corpse, eating one, a stolen one petrifying the nymph, a corpse thrown into an animal engulfer or dropped inside one (`engulfer_digests_food()`), monsters touching a cockatrice (`attk_protection()`), pets keeping away from cockatrices and their corpses, and Medusa's gaze (an amulet of reflection turns it back; m15 step 6: Medusa is in the roster, and her gaze stones the monsters that meet it, `gazemm()`). Every path leaves a statue (see Statues) whose `corpseOf` is the monster and whose contents are the monster's things (`containedIn`; it is not a container the hero can open), historic for a unique monster, and the square forgets its `I`. Golems turn into stone golems (`mon_to_stone()`, and a hero in a golem form: m13 step 6). Monsters cure themselves first with a lizard or acidic corpse or a potion of acid (`munstone()`, m14 step 6: "seems limber!", stomach acid that may kill). A shifted vampire reverts instead (`vamp_stone()`, m18 step 2). Not ported: the new moon (no clock), `selftouch()`, monsters wielding cockatrice corpses (`select_hwep()`, `mselftouch()`), monsters bumping into one while hurtling, monster displacement, the hero's statue (no bones), cockatrice eggs (m9 step 8 or m10), the polymorphed hero's touch and stoning (7f); the killer of a stack of cockatrice corpses reads as a single one | No clock, no polymorph or muse.c yet (ref: src/uhitm.c mhitm_ad_ston(), do_stone_u(), do_stone_mon(), passive(), hmon_hitmon_misc_obj(); src/timeout.c stoned_dialogue(); src/trap.c instapetrify(), minstapetrify(); src/mon.c monstone()) |

| Statues | A statue shows the monster it depicts (C 3.6+ statue glyphs, male and female ones apart), in the statue's colour; `mksobj(STATUE)` rolls a random monster (`rndmonnum()`) even when the caller gives one, and a gender (`CORPSTAT_MALE`/`FEMALE`); the historic flag shows only to an Archeologist ("historic statue of ...") and breaking a historic statue makes one feel guilty; "empty statue" once its contents are known; statue traps; fracturing a shop's statue is billed; applying or tipping one ("Nothing interesting happens."); hallucination shows a random monster and remembers a random object | 7f-c: each vanilla monster has a statue appearance (`nh:statue-<monster>`, its letter in white) that `ObjectDef.appearanceOf` gives the view, object memory and Look, and a hallucinating hero sees a random monster (memory keeps the statue); a new statue (`mksobjStatue()`) rolls the random monster and spellbook (`rn2(level_difficulty() / 2 + 10) > 10`, not for a very small monster) only when made with init or without a monster, and no gender (genders are not modelled, as for corpses); the historic flag is kept (`modData.nh.historic`; a stoned unique monster's statue gets it, as `monstone()`) and named and felt only by an Archeologist (no such role yet); Look names a statue in view ("a statue of a gnome", a live gnome is "gnome"), but a remembered one out of sight only by the floor it is on (Look names no remembered object; C's `lookat()` names the statue from its glyph); striking, force bolt, a pick-axe and a blast break one (`break_statue()`); kicking one is "Thump!"; a big statue does not fit into a container; no "empty" prefix (no `cknown` for statues), shop billing for a fracture or #apply/#tip; statue traps: see Statue traps | Needs systems not ported (ref: src/mkobj.c mksobj_init(), mksobj(), mkcorpstat(); src/zap.c break_statue(); src/objnam.c xname(); src/display.c map_glyphinfo(), newsym()) |
| Statue traps | `mk_trap_statue()` makes a statue of `rndmonnum_adj(3, 6)` holding the monster's inventory; stepping on the trap, searching next to it, kicking, striking, force bolt or digging the statue, or a blast (deletes it) set it off: `animate_statue()` restores a stoned monster's saved traits (`omonst`), turns a golem's statue hit by stone to flesh into a flesh golem, a unique monster's or a quest guardian's into a doppelganger, names the monster after a named statue, reveals mimics, and charges shop statues | 7f-c: the statue trap (hidden until it goes off; trap detection shows it, secret door detection does not), made from level difficulty 8 with its statue (a monster made and removed so its starting gear goes inside; a co-aligned unicorn rerolled); it goes off when she steps on it (also floating; not when she floats down onto it), finds it by searching (it is not "found"), kicks the statue, hits it with striking or force bolt (from `bhitpile()` before the pile, then in `break_statue()`) or a pick-axe; the first statue there comes to life awake and hostile (`animate_statue()`: "The statue comes to life!" under her, "Instead of shattering, the statue of a jackal suddenly comes to life!", "You find a jackal posing as a statue.", "disappears" for one she cannot see, "moves" for a nonliving one, a random monster "suddenly seems more animated" while hallucinating), next to her when she stands there, with the statue's contents as its inventory, which it puts on; a shop's statue animated by a blow or the spell is charged (`stolen_value()`), a trap's is not; an Archeologist feels guilty about a historic statue. | Stone to flesh | The spell (level 3 healing, "thick") animates statues and figurines, turns boulders, stones, gems, stone rings and the marble wand into meat (`poly_obj()`), stone golems into flesh golems, reveals stone-mimicking mimics, cures her stoning (and a stone golem form) when cast at herself and turns her stone objects to meat, smoothes engravings, bleeds from the ceiling or onto the floor; non-meat eaters "smell the odor of meat", meat eaters "a delicious smell" | 7f-c: the spellbook (objects.h's, from the converter) and its spell through the zap code: a statue comes to life ("The statue of a jackal comes to life!"), or becomes a meatball for a monster not made of flesh, or its monster's corpse (contents dropped) when it cannot come to life and leaves corpses; a boulder becomes an enormous meatball (Sokoban costs Luck), rocks, gray stones and gems meatballs, the stone rings a meat ring (a worn one comes off), a wand of make invisible a meat stick; each object resists 2 times in 100 (`obj_resists(obj, 2, 98)`, no artifacts); a weapon slot carries over, the quantity stays (1 for a meat ring); a shop's goods changed this way anger the keeper, unpaid ones stay on the bill as used up. At herself it cures stoning ("You feel limber!") and hits her whole pack, then merges what it can; up: "Blood drips on your face."; down on an empty square: "Blood pools at your feet." (beneath them when floating). The smell is always "You smell the odor of meat." (no vegetarian conduct is kept). Stone golems turn to flesh golems and a golem's statue animates as a flesh golem (m13 step 6). A figurine animates where it lies (next to her when she carries it; none when a monster stands on it), a golem's as a flesh golem, a fleshless monster's becomes a meatball (m13 step 7). No mimics, engravings, air or water levels, quest home, artifacts or polymorphed forms | Those objects and monsters do not exist; no conduct (ref: src/zap.c stone_to_flesh_obj(), poly_obj(), bhito(), bhitm(), zapyourself(), zap_updown()) |
| Polymorph | Monsters, objects and the hero polymorph (wand, spell, potion, trap, ring of polymorph, polymorphitis), with polymorph control, Unchanging, system shock and the hero's forms; `#monster` uses a form's special ability | Built in 7f-d. Step 1: the wand, spell (level 6 matter) and potion of polymorph, the rings of polymorph and polymorph control, the amulets of change and unchanging, the polymorph trap (from level 8, bright green; magic resistance or Unchanging: "You feel momentarily different." and the trap stays, else it is used up: "You feel a change coming over you.") and the hero's gender (`modData.nh.ugender`). Step 2, monsters and objects: `newcham()` picks a random form as 3.7 does (one uniform pick over the monster table per try, 20 tries of `accept_newcham_form()`: no placeholders, nothing `M2_NOPOLY`; `validspecmon()`'s shopkeeper/priest restriction only matters on the Rogue level, as in 3.7), rolls the level and hit points for it keeping the old fraction, keeps tameness, inventory and a shopkeeper's or priest's role (a polymorphed watchman is no longer of the Watch), drops a weapon the form cannot use and armor it cannot wear (`mon_break_armor()`: "The dwarf breaks out of his armor!", "The dwarf's cloak tears apart!", "... armor falls around him!", helmets, gloves, shields, boots; also for a lycanthrope changing form), lets an engulfed or held hero go, and says "The jackal turns into a gnome!", "... disappears!" or "A gnome appears!"; the wand and spell: magic resistance shields, else unless it resists, 1 in 25 system shock ("The jackal shudders!", killed with no corpse), else `newcham()`; what the monster drops is not hit by the same zap. A monster on a polymorph trap changes unless it resists (the trap stays); iron shoes on the trap (hers or a monster's) warp into kicking boots and back ("Your pair of hard shoes warps strangely."). `poly_obj()`: a random object of the same class (magic-or-not kept in 3 tries), quantity, BUC, charges, recharge count, erosion and box traps kept, contents lost, no wand of wishing or polymorph, spellbooks degrade to blank paper, mineral gems may backfire into rocks, statues become statues of other monsters or boulders, shop goods anger the keeper; polypiling: the polymorph objects and the amulet of unchanging never change, anything else resists 5 in 100, wands and cursed objects shudder 1 in 3 (blessed 1 in 12, others 1 in 8, halved over 4 of a kind) and are destroyed ("You feel shuddering vibrations.", some of a stack survive, shop goods billed); boulders are put back on top of the pile. Polypiling melds a pile into a golem (`create_polymon()`, `polyuse()`: m13 step 6). Vampires and vampire lords are shapeshifters since m18 step 2 (see Vampires (m18)): a vampire's own random pick (`pickvampshape()`), its natural form accepted, a polymorph that makes a vampire makes a vampshifter, a shapeshifter is not immune as the Riders and the birth-limited are, system shock spares it and a failed zap reverts it. Since m18 step 3 (see Shapeshifters (m18)) chameleons, doppelgangers and sandestins pick their forms as `select_newcham_form()` does, an ordinary monster in dragon scales or scale mail becomes that dragon, the random form leaves out the player monsters (C's LOW_PM..SPECIAL_PM), a player monster is accepted only as a doppelganger's pick, a cancelled shapeshifter is uncancelled by a polymorph (not under protection from shape changers), cancellation forces a shapeshifter back for good, genocide reverts or kills shapeshifters, and eating a chameleon or doppelganger corpse polymorphs her ("You feel a change coming over you.", "You feel momentarily different." with Unchanging). Not ported: the Rogue level, monster genders (no sex change on a monster's polymorph), mimics, long worms, hiding monsters, steeds, eggs, leashes, poisoned ammo, artifacts, polypile conduct (a magic lamp comes out an oil lamp with 1500 turns of oil, a magic marker recharged once and a potion of oil's fuel is fixed up as C's `fixup_oil()`, since m19 step 1); magic resistance's shield effect is not drawn. Step 3, the hero: `polyself()` from the wand, spell, potion (blessed: forced low control, the form capped at `rn2(15) + 10` turns), trap, polymorphitis (1 turn in 100 with Polymorph) and wizard `#polyself`; Unchanging "You fail to transform!"; system shock `rn2(20) > Con` without control ("You shudder for a moment.", rnd(30)); the control prompt "Become what kind of monster? [type the name]" (a name, a one-letter class, "*"/"random", 5 tries, "I've never heard of such monsters.", "You can't polymorph into a werejackal.", "That's enough tries!"; Escape under forced control is "Never mind."); a random polyok() form or 1 in 5 (never under forced control) `newman()`. `polymon()`: "You turn into a gnome lord!" (a sex change of a form of either sex: "a male gnome lord"), `rn1(500, 500)` turns scaled by her level over the form's, the form's d8-per-level hit points (D6: in `c.hp`, hers parked), speed, strength rule (a strong form's race maximum, else capped), properties (resistances, see invisible, telepathy, teleportitis and control, levitation for floaters, regeneration, stun for bats, blindness for eyeless forms), the form's letter on the map (D4 `self`), `HD:n` on the status line, release from a holder or an engulfer that can't hold the form, bear traps and webs for small or amorphous forms. Returning: the timeout, the form's hit points running out from any damage ("You return to human form!"), Unchanging keeping the form (dying in it: "killed while stuck in creature form"; "Your amulet fails!"). `newman()`: level -2..+2 (below 1 kills: "Your new form doesn't seem healthy enough to survive."), peak level, 1 in 10 sex change, experience, Str/Dex/Con/Cha by -2..+2, hit points and energy rerolled, hunger, sickness and stoning cured, "You feel like a new woman!". Lycanthropy: an uncontrolled polyself or 1 turn in 80 (no clock, so never the night's 1 in 60) turns her into her beast unless a hostile monster is next to her; with control "Do you want to change into a jackal?"; the beast's time out returns her ("Remain in beast form?" with control, else rn1(200, 200) more turns); unholy water changes her and holy water purifies and returns her. Level gain and drain change both pools (monhp_per_lvl(): rnd(4) for every form, as C's unset youmonst.m_lev makes it); an undead, demon or were form resists drain; prayer's TROUBLE_HIT only when not polymorphed (or Unchanging); the sasquatch form kicks as a martial artist. Not ported in step 3: dragon armor, vampires, genocide, light-emitting forms, mimicking and hiding, sticking to monsters, pools, slime, slippery fingers, strangulation by form, eggs, retouch_equipment(); `redist_attr()` varies the stored attribute (no AMAX); `regen_hp()` for a form ignores `u.umoved`. Step 4, what a form can wear, wield, carry and eat: `break_armor()` (a big form breaks out of its suit and rips its shirt, both destroyed, and its cloak's clasp breaks open (dropped; a mummy wrapping tears apart unless the form may wear it); a small, whirly or noncorporeal form lets suit, cloak and shirt fall (a hobbit keeps an elven mithril-coat); horns push off a helmet and pierce one of leather or softer; a handless or tiny form drops gloves with its weapon, shield and helmet; a handless, tiny, slithy or centaur form loses its boots; a headless one its blindfold; rings and the amulet stay) and `drop_weapon()` ("You find you must drop your spear!", a welded one is released and kept); `canwearobj()` ("You can't wear any armor in your current form.", "The suit will not fit on your body." with C's cloak exception, horns, "You have no feet...", hooves), rings ("You cannot make the ring stick to your body.") and eyewear ("You have no head to wear a blindfold on."); wielding ("Don't be ridiculous!", the swap and wield-nothing offers, `wield_tool()`), applying, zapping, throwing (`notake` or handless) and opening doors (also by walking into one) need hands; a `notake` form picks nothing up; the offers follow these rules; eating by diet (yummy and palatable from the form's carnivore/herbivore flags, a vegetarian corpse is always just "okay", tripe for a carnivorous non-humanoid or an orc, lembas for an orc or elf, lembas and cram nutrition for elf, orc and dwarf forms, "This tastes just like chicken!", the raven's eyeball, a ghoul form eats only meaty corpses: "You cannot eat that!"); `weight_cap()` from the form (a nymph carries the most, a form without a corpse weight by its size, a weak or heavy form by its weight against a human's); Flying from flying forms (blocked by levitation or being stuck in the floor: over floor traps "You fly over a pit.", no fall into a trap door or hole unless she plunged ("You swoop down through the trap door!", no fall damage), a huge form "don't fit through", a dug pit or hole under her, pit traps, `immune_to_trap()` for the trap prompt, wounded legs and carrying capacity, "Really fly onto...", a nymph "snatches beneath you", a short monster "tries to reach your leg", enlightenment "You are flying."/"You would fly if..."); a clinging form does not fall through a hole; Infravision from infravision forms (an infravisible monster in her line of sight is shown in the dark, enlightenment "You have infravision."). Not ported in step 4: non-food meals of metallivore, gelatinous cube and fire elemental forms (`doeat_nonfood()`), the autopickup message "You are physically incapable of picking anything up." (no autopickup option: a notake form's pick-up only looks), body part words for forms ("ring-finger", "feet" are always a human's), the form's own locomotion verbs ("slither"), clinging forms over pits, swimming and passing walls, `cancel_don()`, shop selling of dropped armor, exercise; infravision does not change content's `canseemon()` (messages still treat a monster seen only by infravision as unseen); locks, #force and looting do not check hands yet. Step 5: fighting as a form (`hmonas()`): each of the form's attacks rolls to hit with the form's level and `adj_lev()`-based to-hit bonus (no Str damage bonus while polymorphed), weapon and claw attacks of a form with hands go through the wielded weapon, the others through the damage types' hero branches (fire, cold, shock, sleep, poison, paralysis, draining, teleporting, blinding, theft, petrification...), the target's passive answers each blow (a bite into a cockatrice petrifies), exploding forms explode and return to human form, engulfing forms swallow for one round (a purple worm form digests at once), a form with passive attacks only "has no way to attack monsters physically", forms kick with their kick attacks and limbless, tiny or lizard forms cannot kick; monsters hit the form's own AC, can't grab an unsolid form or kick a thick-skinned one, and the form's passive answers them (acid, paralysing gaze or touch, cold that heals and may split her, stun, fire, shock, a gas spore form exploding); enlightenment "You are polymorphed into a jaguar." and "a werejackal in beast form". Not ported in step 5: the hero holding a monster (a lichen, python or owlbear form's sticky touch, wrap or hug does its damage without holding), two-weapon alternation for forms with several weapon attacks, silver and blessed bonuses (`special_dmgval()`), shades, knockback, mind flayer `AD_DRIN`, demon forms summoning (`demonpet()`: no polymorphable demon), `m_slips_free()` (no greased armor), monster reflection of a floating eye form's gaze, stealing a cockatrice corpse (`theft_petrifies()`), christening the copy of a split form, the wizard-mode form timer in enlightenment (forms with breath, spit or gaze attacks don't use them in melee, as NetHack). Step 6, `#monster` (`domonability()`, `M-m`), offered on herself only while her form has an ability ported here: a breath weapon (15 energy, spent even if the direction is then cancelled; "You don't have enough energy to breathe!", "You can't breathe.  Sorry." while strangled; a ray of the breath's type and dice, "The blast of frost hits the gnome!", resisted at her level, her kill; at herself the blast hits her with no to-hit roll), spitting (blinding venom, else acid venom, flying as a thrown object: it hits when her Dex beats `rnd(25)` (always inside an engulfer), "The venom blinds the gnome!" for `rn1(25, 21)` turns and angers it, and always breaks where it lands: "Splash!"), a fiery or confusing gaze (not blind or hallucinating; 15 energy; every monster she sees in her line of sight: "You attack the gnome with a fiery gaze!" for 2d6 unless it resists fire, its items burning when her level beats `rn2(20)`; a pet is avoided, a peaceful one asked about ("Really attack the gnome?"), one that can't see her invisible doesn't notice, a sleeping, paralysed, stunned, blind or eyeless one is unaffected; a floating eye freezes her unless she has free action), a lycanthrope beast's call for help (10 energy, "You call upon your brethren for help!", 1 to 5 tame helpers, "But none arrive."), a gremlin form multiplying on a fountain (which may dry up; "There is no fountain here.", no time) and a shrieker form's "You shriek." waking the level (no time). Cancellation returns a polymorphed hero to her own form ("Your amulet grows hot for a moment, then cools." with Unchanging). Death texts follow her sex ("zapped himself with a wand of fire", "his own pick-axe"). Not ported in step 6: a nymph form's removal of an iron ball (no ball and chain), hiding, mimicking, spinning webs (a spider form is caught in webs like any other), mind blasts, unicorn horns, vampires, steeds; NetHack's "Any special ability you may have is purely reflexive." (no offer for a form without one); spitting up, down or at herself (the eight directions only); Medusa's gaze; exercise; a silver-hating form handling silver objects (`retouch_object()`: it wields and wears them unharmed; silver damage and "harmed by silver" exist since m11 step 2) | No ball and chain exist (golem forms came with m13 step 6: golemhp() hit points, `ugolemeffects()`, burning, rusting and rotting away, a clay golem form cancelled) (ref: src/polyself.c polyself(), polymon(), polyman(), rehumanize(), newman(), set_uasmon(), change_sex(); src/were.c you_were(), you_unwere(); src/allmain.c moveloop_core(), regen_hp(); src/timeout.c nh_timeout(); src/trap.c trapeffect_poly_trap(); src/potion.c peffect_polymorph(); src/do_wear.c Amulet_on(); src/mon.c newcham(), select_newcham_form(); src/worn.c mon_break_armor(); src/zap.c bhitm(), bhito(), poly_obj(), obj_shudders(), do_osshock(), create_polymon(); src/uhitm.c hmonas(), damageum(), explum(), gulpum(); src/mhitu.c passiveum(); src/cmd.c domonability(); src/polyself.c dobreathe(), dospit(), dogaze(), dosummon(); src/zap.c ubreatheu(), cancel_monst()) |
| Level drain and lycanthropy (m9 step 7) | `losexp()` takes a level (wraiths, barrow wights, vampires, Stormbringer, the spell of drain life, thrones, angry gods, fire traps...) and restore ability, blessed full healing, prayer and a wraith corpse give lost ones back; adjabil() keeps intrinsics that came from elsewhere (FROMEXPER vs FROMOUTSIDE bits); a lycanthrope resists drain, hates silver and turns into her beast 1 turn in 80 (`you_were()`, also unholy water), summons help with `#monster`, and is cured by prayer, holy water or a sprig of wolfsbane; were creatures change form 1 time in 50 (3 or 30 at night or full moon; Protection_from_shape_changers stops it), `new_were()` breaks their armor and makes them flee a scary square; silver hurts them more; vampires shapeshift | The wraith's touch and the barrow wight's blade (AD_DRLI, both directions: "Farvel level N." as C's Valkyrie `Goodbye()`, "The jackal becomes weaker!"), the angry god's "Thou must relearn thy lessons!" and the fire trap's too-low maximum (`losexp(NULL)`), wizard `#levelchange`; the hit points and energy of each level are recorded by `pluslvl()` (`modData.nh.uhpinc`/`ueninc`, keyed by level; a level gained before m9 step 7 falls back to the average gain above the starting 16 HP / 2 Pw), `u.ulevelmax` is stored only while above the current level ("Welcome back"), blessed full healing, prayer's golden glow and the wraith corpse restore; the skill slot goes with `lose_weapon_skill()` (advances made before step 7 have no `u.skill_record` and are not taken back); a lost level takes its intrinsic away even when something else gave it too (no FROMEXPER bits). Lycanthropy: AD_WERE bites ("You feel feverish."), human-form were corpses, drain resistance, enlightenment ("You are a wererat.", "level-drain resistant"), prayer's TROUBLE_LYCANTHROPE and holy water cures; were creatures change form in the monster turn pass (daytime rates, no moon) and when attacking (`summonmu()`: 1 in 5 to beast, 1 in 30 back, 1 in 10 summoning 1-5 rats, jackals or wolves; the winter wolf is not in the roster, its roll is drawn), drop their weapon as beasts, die in their human form. Since m11 step 2 silver harms them (see Silver) and a sprig of wolfsbane cures her. Since m18 step 3 the ring of protection from shape changers keeps were creatures in human form (a beast changes back at once, also when it attacks or is hit by holy or unholy water), stops their summoning (not hers) and keeps her from catching lycanthropy. Not ported: the hero turning into her beast (`you_were()`: its per-turn `rn2(80)` is not drawn, unholy water does nothing more), `#monster`, vampires, thrones, the drain life spell, Stormbringer, restore ability and gain level (not in the object table), `mon_break_armor()` and fleeing a scary square after a change; experience for killing a were creature uses the form it died in | Hero polymorph is 7f-d; silver, vampires and the missing objects are later work (ref: src/exper.c losexp(), pluslvl(); src/attrib.c adjabil(); src/weapon.c lose_weapon_skill(); src/uhitm.c mhitm_ad_drli(), mhitm_ad_were(); src/were.c; src/mhitu.c summonmu(); src/eat.c cpostfx(); src/pray.c; src/potion.c peffect_water(), peffect_full_healing(); src/mon.c mondead(), m_calcdistress()) |

| Travel path shape | `findtravelpath()` takes any shortest path (its search order decides which), so a travel across a room can bend more than needed | Among the shortest paths, the one with the fewest changes of direction | Player feedback: zigzags looked like wasted moves on phones (ref: src/hack.c findtravelpath()) |
| Trap memory | A seen trap's `^` is part of the remembered glyph, so it stays on the map until the cell is seen again, even after the trap is gone | A trap is shown while it exists and `seen` is set; a trap removed out of sight disappears from the map at once | Traps are a level layer with a seen flag rather than remembered glyphs (ref: src/display.c map_trap(), src/trap.c deltrap()) |
| Traps: the hero | `dotrap()` handles Flying, Fumbling, steeds, polymorphed forms and traps the hero set; `water_damage()` also fades scrolls and spellbooks, dilutes potions, wets containers and towels and puts out lamps; a trap's dart may be poisoned (`opoisoned`); `fall_through()` drops the objects on the square too (`impact_drop()`), and the level change is deferred to the end of the move; holes lead no further than the dungeon's bottom | The twenty traps (arrow, dart, falling rock, squeaky board, bear trap, land mine, rolling boulder trap, sleeping gas, rust, pit, spiked pit, trap door, hole, teleportation trap, level teleporter, magic trap, anti-magic field, web, statue trap (see Statues) and polymorph trap (see Polymorph)) with the seen-trap escape (1 in 5; never for an anti-magic field), the hero's helmet, iron shoes and sleep resistance; the rust trap only erodes the worn or wielded item (scrolls, potions, books and containers are spared) and names it by its full name instead of "helmet"; the dart roll decides only whether the hit poisons (the dart left on the floor is never poisoned); the hero falls at once with the trap door's fall damage (`d(dist, 6)`), objects stay behind; a hole's destination is the next level plus one more for each 1 in 4, down to the dungeon's bottom at most; the Castle's lead to Gehennom (see Dungeon bottom); a bear trap ends a run or travel. Pits: "You fall into a pit!" (`rnd(6)`; spiked `rnd(10)`, poisoned 1 in 6, iron shoes protect), `rn1(6, 2)` tries to climb out ("You are still in a pit.", "You crawl to the edge of the pit."), moving or `<` tries, `>` on a seen pit plunges in, no kicking ("There's not enough room to kick down here.") or opening doors ("You can't reach over the edge of the pit.") from inside; in a pit the hero still sees the whole room (vision.c limits her to the adjacent squares), lock picking is not refused. Conjoined pits (dug from inside a pit, see Digging): from a pit she steps into a seen pit next to it without climbing out, "You move into an adjacent pit." (no damage, `rnd(4)` on spikes) when they are conjoined, else "You stumble over debris." (`rnd(3)`, `rnd(6)` on spikes); never the seen-trap escape. NetHack 3.7's `domove_core()` passes no destination trap to `trapmove()`, so there she must climb out first and the conjoined cases never happen; this port follows 3.6's `domove()` (and the kickoff), which passes it. Death reasons read "killed by falling into a pit" (NetHack's NO_KILLER_PREFIX "fell into a pit"). A rolling boulder trap gets its boulder when made (`mkroll_launch()`: a spot 2..8 squares away in a line with a clear path to the trap and as far beyond it; none in Sokoban or where no line fits, then no boulder): "Click!  You trigger a rolling boulder trap!" and the boulder rolls from its spot across the trap to the far end (`launch_obj()`), hitting monsters in its way (a hit stops it; the kill is hers only on a trap she had seen) and her (`thitu(9 + spe)`, `rnd(20)`), filling pits and holes, vanishing on a teleportation trap (to a random spot of the level) or a level teleporter (gone: objects never migrate here), setting another boulder rolling, breaking closed doors, stopping before walls, rock and trees ("Thump!") and iron bars ("Whang!"); with no boulder, "Fortunately for you, no boulder was released." ("No boulder was released." on a trap she knew) and the trap stays, as in NetHack 3.7; giants never snatch the boulder; a land mine on its way goes off 7 times in 10 (the boulder breaks apart, the blast scatters the rocks, the mine is gone). A web (m10): "You stumble into a spider web!" ("float into" while floating: a web is not set off by the floor, so floating does not pass over it; "You are caught by a spider web!" when forced), stuck for a time by Strength (`rn1(6, 6)` at 3 down to `rnd(2)` at 15-17, 1 at 18 to 18/50; from 18/51 "You tear through a web!" and it is gone), each move takes a turn off ("You are stuck to the web.", "You disentangle yourself."), no kicking ("You can't move your leg!") or digging ("Unfortunately, you can't dig while entangled in a web."); a pick swung at a web gets entangled (`d(2, 2)` turns, "You pull free."); a seen web next to her can be force-fought (`F` or the Fight offer): a blade cuts through (`rn2(20)` against `acurrstr() - 2 + spe + skill - 2`), bare hands punch through (`rn2(45 - 5 * (skill - 2))`), any other weapon "can't cut a web with a club!", a success trains the skill; fire rays and explosions burn webs ("A web bursts into flames!"), freeing whoever was caught; a thrown or kicked object flying over a web gets stuck in it 1 time in 3 ("The dagger gets stuck in a web!"); being engulfed releases her ("You are released from the web!"); she is never a web-walker (a spider form is caught like any other), there is no Sting or fire artifact, steed, sitting (`#sit` is not ported for any trap) or ball and chain, and she cannot throw herself out (`hurtle()` is not ported). A land mine: "KAABLAMM!!!  You triggered a land mine!", `rnd(16)` damage (a quarter in iron shoes), wounded legs for `rn1(35, 41)` turns (both legs in NetHack; one wounded-legs timeout here, set twice), the objects there scatter (`scatter(4)`: they fly up to 4 squares, may hit monsters and her, break 1 time in 10 if breakable, boulders break apart), monsters within 20 squares wake, the mine becomes a seen pit and she falls into it (the seen-trap escape applies); a boulder lying there fills it. Floating, she passes over it (NetHack 3.7 lets a floating hero discover the trigger and set it off 1 time in 3 only when forced); no one sets land mines (`use_trap()`), so none is ever hers. A teleportation trap teleports her at random within the level ("You feel a wrenching sensation." with magic resistance or where teleporting is barred; a trap she lands on does not go off); a level teleporter is used up and level-teleports her at random ("You step onto a level teleport trap!", "You feel disoriented." and 3 turns of confusion; "You trigger..." and no magic-resistance save when forced), with the deferred goto's "You materialize on a different level!" after those messages; a once-only teleportation trap (the vault teleporter, see Vaults) is used up and takes her into the vault (a monster too, and the trap stays); no fixed-destination teleporters, no endgame. A magic trap explodes 1 time in 30 (`rnd(10)` damage, +2 maximum energy, the trap gone), else picks one of NetHack's twenty fates, with one not modelled: the flash and roar's deafness (the roar is only its message; monsters still appear and wake); with no Quest she always yearns for her "distant homeland"; fate 12 is the fire trap's tower of flame (`dofiretrap()`: no fire trap kind exists, NetHack makes them only in Gehennom), whose loss of maximum HP below her level only floors it there (no level drain, `losexp()` is not ported). An anti-magic field drains `d(2,6)` energy (`drain_en()`), turns into `rnd(4)` damage with magic resistance (no Half_physical/spell_damage, Magicbane or artifacts), and disenchants positively enchanted iron shoes instead. Floating, she passes over every trap but the teleporters, the magic trap and the anti-magic field ("You float over a pit." when it is seen; no confirmation for the ground traps), except Sokoban's pits and holes; a forced trap door does not drop her ("You don't fall in."). `#untrap` (offered on herself while a seen trap, a box at her feet not known to be safe or a closed door is within reach, with NetHack's direction question, "here" included, and directly on an adjacent seen trap or closed door; `Alt+u`): `untrap_prob()` (1 in 3, worse while confused, hallucinating, blind or stunned), arrow and dart traps leave `50 - rnl(50)` arrows or darts, a bear trap and a land mine become a beartrap and a land mine object; failing, 4 times in 5 with Luck 0 "Whoops..." sets the trap off (under her it goes off; next to her she moves onto it unless the move is not possible), else "That bear trap is difficult to disarm."; a squeaky board needs a potion of oil or a can of grease and neither object exists, so "You don't have anything to untrap with." and no time (NetHack still asks and lets her pick any potion, which always fails); a pit says "Try filling the pit instead." unless a trapped monster is in it (see Traps: monsters); a web is removed (`untrap_prob()` 1 in 7 bare-handed or with a monster in it, 1 in 3 with a blade wielded: "You cut through the web." / "You succeed in removing the web.", "That web is difficult to remove."; a monster in it is extracted, and failing with one in it, 1 time in 3 the web spreads to her square, "The web sticks to you.  You're caught too!", "The jackal remains entangled."); the other kinds cannot be disabled; caught in a trap she cannot deal with it; floating she cannot reach the floor; no Fumbling, Master Key of Thievery, mimics or steeds, and no exercise | Flying forms: see Polymorph; no steeds (Sokoban's inescapable pits and holes: see Sokoban); poisoned objects are not modelled; deafness and level drain are not modelled; one dungeon (ref: src/trap.c dotrap(), trapeffect_*(), mkroll_launch(), launch_obj(), domagictrap(), dofiretrap(), drain_en(), water_damage(), fall_through(), hole_destination(), src/do.c goto_level(), src/teleport.c tele_trap(), level_tele_trap()) |
| Traps: monsters | `mintrap()` also handles metallivores eating bear traps, hiders, steeds and traps the hero set; `mons_see_trap()` skips blinded monsters; a falling monster migrates and, on arrival, `mon_arrive()` catches up the elapsed time; body parts come from `mbodypart()` | The same eighteen traps on monsters (a land mine goes off under a monster unless `rn2(weight + 1) < 400` ("KAABLAMM!!!  The hill orc triggers a land mine!", "Kaablamm!  You hear an explosion in the distance!" unseen; a flyer forced onto it 1 time in 3, "The air currents set it off!"): `rnd(16)`, the blast as for the hero, it falls into the new pit; its kills are no one's), missile to-hit with `find_mac()`, a rolling boulder trap's boulder ("Click!  The jackal triggers a rolling boulder trap." when seen, "You hear rumbling in the distance." when not; its kills are no one's; harmless in Sokoban), bear traps holding medium and bigger monsters, pits holding everything that walks (`rnd(6)`, spiked `rnd(10)`; out 1 in 40 per turn, huge monsters climb out "easily" at once; a monster in a pit makes no kick attack), sleep, rust on worn armor and weapons, squeaky boards waking monsters within 40; webs (m10): caught ("The jackal is caught in a spider web.", an owlbear or bugbear out of sight roars), out 1 in 40 per turn ("pulls free of the web"), giants and the big ones (purple worm, titanothere, ...) tear through, amorphous, whirly and unsolid monsters and the gelatinous cube flow through and fiery or acidic ones burn or dissolve it, cave and giant spiders walk webs and spin new ones after they move (NetHack 3.7's `maybe_spin_web()`: `(giant ? 15 : 5) * (walls + 1) - 3 * webs` in 1000, `d(4, 4)` turns before the next, Sokoban only within sight of the up stairs, shop damage the keeper removes; never in a doorway, where core keeps traps off, nor does a failed #untrap spread one there), a giant spider made in a room sleeps on a web; a teleportation trap moves it at random within the level, nothing on a no-teleport level ("The jackal seems disoriented."/"suddenly disappears!"); a level teleporter sends it, confused, to a random level from the hero's ("Suddenly, the jackal disappears out of sight."; "shudders for a moment" when that is this level), and neither trap spares flyers; a magic trap burns it 1 time in 21 (the tower of flame: `d(2,4)` and as much maximum HP, burnt armor and items, no golems), an anti-magic field delays a non-resistant caster's or breather's next spell by `d(2,6)` turns ("The gnomish wizard seems lethargic.") and hurts a magic-resistant one `rnd(4)` ("killed by the compression from an anti-magic field")); a monster that falls through a trap door or hole, or level-teleports, leaves the map (no position) until the hero arrives on that level, then comes out at a random free square (one uniform pick after 50 tries instead of rloc()'s shuffle) without catching up; rust messages say "forelimb" for animals and "arm" for everyone else; `#untrap` next to a trapped monster: one in a bear trap is extracted ("You extract the newt from the bear trap.", the trap stays) or, failing with "Whoops...", bitten for `rnd(4)` (a pet is abused); one in a pit is pulled out (`help_monster_out()`: it may back away skeptically, slip from her grasp, wake or stir, be too heavy for her, and the pit is filled if a boulder lies there); a hostile one helped out may become peaceful ("The jackal is grateful."), and a lawful hero may "feel that you did the right thing" (`reward_untrap()`); no cockatrices to touch | Roster and systems: no metallivores, hiders, steeds, blinded monsters, golems or wall passers (Sokoban: see Sokoban) (ref: src/trap.c mintrap(), thitm(), trapeffect_magic_trap(), trapeffect_fire_trap(), trapeffect_anti_magic(), src/teleport.c mtele_trap(), mlevel_tele_trap(), rloc(), src/mondata.c mons_see_trap(), src/polyself.c mbodypart()) |
| Stepping onto a seen trap | With `paranoid_confirm:trap` (on by default) "Really step onto that X?" unless stunned or confused or the trap is clearly harmless; while hallucinating a random trap is named; the `m` prefix skips it | Always asked as NetHack's default does, cancellable; a rust trap is harmless when nothing rustprone is worn; the real trap is named even while hallucinating; there is no `m` prefix; travel never routes through a seen trap and running stops in front of one | No options or `m` prefix yet (ref: src/hack.c avoid_trap_andor_region(), immune_to_trap(), test_move() TEST_TRAV/TEST_TRAP) |

("You don't feel like yourself." under Unchanging), and disintegrates; the rings of polymorph and polymorph control and the amulet of unchanging confer their properties, see Polymorph (a limbless form can't put on a ring: "You cannot make the ring stick to your body."); stealth announces itself only when it changes her stealth; warning and conflict: see their rows; the amulet of magical breathing turns choking on a meal into vomiting and changes the strangling texts, and nothing else: no water, gas clouds or breath-related monster attacks exist; it does not stop strangulation, as in NetHack); P and R for accessories only (T stays armor-only), the hero is right-handed; slippery gloves keep rings from going on or coming off and slippery bare fingers lose uncursed rings each turn (m19 step 8: see Towels and slippery fingers); no silver; descriptions and colors are shuffled at game start, but a ring keeps the material of its own (unshuffled) description; generated rings and amulets roll BUC and enchantment as `mksobj_init()` does | Subset; materials follow the def, not the appearance (ref: src/do_wear.c, src/o_init.c shuffle(), src/mkobj.c mksobj_init()) |
